data: {"type":"response.output_item.done","item":{"type":"function_call_output","call_id":"call_afb881dc-5c74-4b72-8364-f56f2d4f8a48","output":"  1  ---\n  2  name: report-builder\n  3  description: Builds a self-contained HTML report from a compact JSON ReportConfig via the build_report tool. Every derived figure is computed by the engine from the evidence, so the report and the evidence cannot disagree. Use when the user asks for a report, a báo cáo, an executive summary as a document, or a shareable link to analysis results.\n  4  ---\n  5  \n  6  # Report Builder\n  7  \n  8  You write a **JSON `ReportConfig`** with two keys — `meta` and `sections` — and\n  9  `build_report` renders it into one self-contained HTML file (sidebar, charts,\n 10  sortable tables, RAG colour, light/dark, print). The template owns all markup,\n 11  styling and colour. Every block is complete where you write it, and the only\n 12  names in it are the evidence's own.\n 13  \n 14  ## The one rule about figures\n 15  \n 16  **A figure you can read off a row, you type. A figure you would have to work\n 17  out, you never type — you hand over a `calc`.** A total, a variance or a share\n 18  typed by hand is a number no evidence supports and nothing downstream can check:\n 19  \n 20  ```json\n 21  { \"label\": \"TOI YTD\", \"value\": { \"agg\": \"sum\", \"col\": \"actual\", \"fmt\": \"ty1\" } }\n 22  ```\n 23  \n 24  not `\"value\": \"10,973.4\"`. The engine sums the same rows the table below shows,\n 25  so headline and components are two views of one sum. Every value slot takes a\n 26  calc: KPI value, `delta`, progress `ratio`, table cell, table `total`.\n 27  \n 28  **Prose is not an exception.** An `insight` carries its own `vars` — the same\n 29  calc objects — and names them in its `html` as `{name}`; the engine derives,\n 30  formats and drops the figure into the sentence at build time:\n 31  \n 32  ```json\n 33  { \"type\": \"insight\",\n 34    \"src\": { \"ev\": \"wi_bank_9f2b\", \"where\": { \"section\": \"ytd\" } },\n 35    \"vars\": { \"toi\": { \"agg\": \"sum\", \"col\": \"actual\", \"fmt\": \"ty1\" },\n 36              \"gap\": { \"expr\": \"a - b\", \"vars\": { \"a\": { \"agg\": \"sum\", \"col\": \"actual\" },\n 37                                                  \"b\": { \"agg\": \"sum\", \"col\": \"budget\" } },\n 38                       \"fmt\": \"signed_ty1\" } },\n 39    \"html\": \"TOI đạt <strong>{toi} tỷ</strong>, {gap} tỷ so với kế hoạch.\" }\n 40  ```\n 41  \n 42  A `{name}` the block does not declare is a build error. A figure typed into a\n 43  sentence has not been through the engine: one replayed turn typed 113 of them.\n 44  An action `body` has no `vars`; derived figures there go in an `insight` beside it.\n 45  \n 46  ## The contract\n 47  \n 48  Every object below is closed: a key not on this card is rejected, and the error\n 49  prints the allowed list for that exact path. This card is the whole contract;\n 50  `contracts/report-config.schema.json` says the same thing in 24 KB.\n 51  \n 52  Read it as: `key*` required · after `|` optional · `∈` the complete set of\n 53  allowed values · `⊕` two exclusive shapes · `→` the key that tells a union's\n 54  members apart.\n 55  \n 56  ```text\n 57  config                meta* sections*\n 58  \n 59  meta                  title* | doc_title label meta_line brand footer lang\n 60  section               id* title* blocks* | label in_nav nav_level ∈ 1 2\n 61  \n 62  src                   ev* | where\n 63  calc  ⊕                   agg* col* | ev where fmt\n 64                            expr* vars* | fmt\n 65                            agg ∈ sum avg min max count first last\n 66  value                     a typed string or number  ⊕  a calc\n 67  fmt                   int ty0 ty1 ty2 pct0 pct1 pct2 signed_ty0 signed_ty1 signed_int usd_mn kh raw\n 68  \n 69  cf                    thresholds* | target else_class\n 70    thresholds[]        op* value* class* | value2\n 71                            op ∈ lt lte gt gte eq between\n 72                            class, else_class ∈ good warn bad up down neutral\n 73  \n 74  blocks[] → type\n 75    insight             type* html* | src vars   (vars: name → calc · html: {name})\n 76    subtitle            type* text* | margin_top\n 77    divider             type*\n 78    kpi_group           type* cards* | src columns\n 79      cards[]           label* value* | unit sub cf delta progress\n 80        delta           value* | suffix dir ∈ positive negative neutral auto\n 81        progress        ratio* | cap state ∈ good warn bad · rule\n 82    driver_row          type* boxes* | src\n 83      boxes[]           val* lbl* | delta\n 84    table               type* columns* | src rows total sortable variant ∈ default product\n 85      columns[]         label* | col align ∈ left right center · fmt cf\n 86      rows[][]          v* | bold align tag ∈ up down warn neutral · cf   ⊕   a value\n 87      total             cells*\n 88    chart               type* id* spec* | title sub size ∈ sm lg\n 89    chart_pair          type* charts*\n 90    actions             type* cards*\n 91      cards[]           priority* title* body* | action_id\n 92                            priority ∈ high medium\n 93        body[]          label* | html items signal\n 94          signal        text* | label\n 95  \n 96  spec → kind\n 97    bar_group | line    kind* src* x* series* | x_rotate y_name value_suffix legend\n 98      series[]          col* | name agg where type ∈ bar line · color label\n 99                            line_style ∈ solid dashed dotted\n100    pie                 kind* src* name_col* value_col* | radius value_suffix legend\n101    afb                 kind* src* month_col* actual* budget* | forecast forecast_band y_name\n102  ```\n103  \n104  Each of these has cost a whole build, or built a wrong figure:\n105  \n106  **Two unions, two different discriminators.** A **block** says `type`; a **chart\n107  spec** says `kind`. Writing `kind` on a block fails at every block at once.\n108  \n109  **A name that fits one level up is still a rejected key.** `title` belongs to a\n110  section, a chart block and an action card, and to nothing else — a `kpi_group`\n111  and a `table` have no title. `text` is `subtitle`'s; `insight` takes `html`.\n112  `items` is an action body's; `kpi_group` and `actions` take `cards`.\n113  \n114  **`x`, `col`, `name_col`, `value_col`, `month_col`, `actual`, `forecast`,\n115  `budget` are all evidence column names** — plain strings, taken from the header\n116  of the evidence that block's `src` names.\n117  \n118  **`fmt` is display only and never rescales.** `pct1` puts a sign after the\n119  number it is given: `3.9` prints `3.9%`, `0.039` prints `0.0%`. A count is `int`,\n120  a count's delta `signed_int`. **`delta.dir` is the colour**, not the direction\n121  that is good: `auto` colours by sign; name one only to override the sign.\n122  \n123  **A ratio column can arrive as a fraction** (`0.039`, `1.005`) while the\n124  sub-agent's answer says `NIM 3.85%`, and nothing but the digest tells you which\n125  you have. Put the scale in the calc, never in a typed number:\n126  `{ \"expr\": \"v * 100\", \"vars\": { \"v\": { \"agg\": \"first\", \"col\": \"nim_pct\" } }, \"fmt\": \"pct1\" }`,\n127  build, and read that line of the digest against the sub-agent's figure — `3.9%`\n128  keeps the `* 100`, `390.0%` drops it. A `src` table column has no calc, so a\n129  fraction column is shown as a `total` cell or an `insight` var, not as a column.\n130  \n131  **`sum` is for flows; a balance is read, not added.** TOI, NII, NFI and fees are\n132  a month's worth each, so a period's figure is the months added up. A balance\n133  (`eop_*`, `*_bal`, AUM) is the amount *as at* a month, and month-end balances\n134  added together are the same money counted once per month. Pin the last month in\n135  `where` and read it with `last`:\n136  `{ \"agg\": \"last\", \"col\": \"eop_bal\", \"where\": { \"rptmonth\": \"202608\" }, \"fmt\": \"ty1\" }`.\n137  `sum` folds a balance across segments at one month, never across months.\n138  \n139  The smallest config that builds:\n140  \n141  ```json\n142  { \"meta\": { \"label\": \"TECHCOMBANK · CIBG\", \"title\": \"CIBG — TOI 5T2026\",\n143              \"meta_line\": \"5T2026 · nguồn: Genie · BF Hub\" },\n144    \"sections\": [\n145      { \"id\": \"overview\", \"label\": \"01 — Tổng quan\", \"title\": \"Tổng quan CIBG\",\n146        \"blocks\": [\n147          { \"type\": \"kpi_group\",\n148            \"src\": { \"ev\": \"wi_cibg_a4c8c2c1-1\", \"where\": { \"section\": \"sector_ytd\" } },\n149            \"cards\": [ { \"label\": \"TOI YTD\", \"unit\": \"tỷ VND\",\n150                         \"value\": { \"agg\": \"sum\", \"col\": \"actual\", \"fmt\": \"ty1\" } } ] } ] } ] }\n151  ```\n152  \n153  ## Evidence, and the rows a block sees\n154  \n155  `src` is `{ \"ev\": \"wi_…\", \"where\": {…} }` and it is the **whole** of what a block\n156  can see. `ev` is the id inside `<evidence id=\"…\">` — not a file path;\n157  `/evidence/wi_x/data.md` is rejected. The engine takes exactly the rows you\n158  describe, so a table of any size costs you these three lines.\n159  \n160  `where` keeps the rows whose column matches: `{\"section\": \"sector_ytd\"}`, or\n161  `{\"sector\": [\"FI\", \"MMU\"]}` for any-of, compared against the value **as the\n162  evidence prints it** — `202601`, not `202601.0`; `FI`, not `Financial Institutions`.\n163  \n164  **A calc inside a block inherits that block's `src`.** Drop `ev` and `where` and\n165  it reads the block's own rows, which is why a total row repeats nothing; a\n166  `where` of its own narrows those rows, and wins on a column both name. A calc\n167  that *names* `ev` has left the block: the block's `where` does not travel with it.\n168  \n169  **One block reads one evidence. There are no joins.** Two evidences meet only\n170  inside an `expr`, as numbers already reduced:\n171  \n172  ```json\n173  { \"expr\": \"cibg / total * 100\",\n174    \"vars\": { \"cibg\":  { \"agg\": \"sum\", \"ev\": \"wi_cibg_a4c8c2c1-1\", \"col\": \"actual\" },\n175              \"total\": { \"agg\": \"sum\", \"ev\": \"wi_bank_9f2b-2\",   \"col\": \"actual\" } } }\n176  ```\n177  \n178  `vars` names are local to that one object — two blocks deriving the same figure\n179  write the same calc twice and still agree, because both read the same rows.\n180  \n181  ## Blocks worth a note\n182  \n183  - **`table` takes rows from exactly one place.** `src` generates one row per row\n184    of the evidence and every column carries `col` — prefer this for anything the\n185    data already holds. `rows` spells them out, for a table that is not a view of\n186    one evidence. Declaring both is an error, and so is declaring neither. There\n187    is no pivot: one row per `data_type` in the evidence is one row per\n188    `data_type` in the table. Actual beside budget for one entity is a\n189    `kpi_group` or `driver_row` whose calcs carry their own `where`.\n190  - **`total` is what a reader checks the headline against.** Its numeric cells are\n191    calcs over the same evidence; the engine drops `ev` and `where` for you.\n192  - **`chart_pair` takes exactly two charts**, each with `size` `sm`.\n193  - **`afb`** draws actual, forecast and budget against one period column: give it\n194    `month_col` plus the column names. `forecast_band` shades the forecast tail.\n195  - **`actions`**: write the recommendation as content for the reader — the\n196    sentences you want printed. `action_id` is optional; the builder stamps one.\n197  - **`cf`** colours a value by threshold. Thresholds are tried in order and the\n198    first hit wins, so write them narrowest-first; `else_class` catches the rest.\n199  \n200  ## Workflow\n201  \n202  1. **Read a preset in `assets/examples/` fully** — it is your template, not an\n203     illustration. `performance.json` is a segment P&L: one evidence, every block\n204     type, `delta` · `progress` · `total` · RAG throughout. Start there.\n205  2. **Plan with `write_todos`, one item per section.** Decide a section's blocks\n206     when you reach it, not before.\n207  3. **Write `meta` and the first section only** to `/report_config.json` with\n208     `write_file` — the smallest config that builds, above, with your names in\n209     it — then call `build_report(config_path=\"/report_config.json\",\n210     filename=…)`. A seven-section config is ~20k tokens; typed once while\n211     reasoning and once in the call it is 40k of a 64k output ceiling, and a\n212     call that reaches the ceiling is cut with nothing saved. One section is a\n213     call the ceiling cannot reach. Then **append one section per `edit_file`**\n214     — `old_string` is the file's last two lines, `  ]` and `}` — and build\n215     again; when an edit removes a key or a column, take its comma too. A report\n216     of one or two sections is a single write.\n217  4. **Read the digest that comes back with the link** — one line per card,\n218     table and chart, by config path, showing the figure as the page prints it.\n219     A unit that contradicts its label, a `—` where a number belongs, a\n220     percentage under 1, a value 1000x off, a balance that dwarfs its own months:\n221     `edit_file` that path, build again. The digest is how you see your `fmt`\n222     and `agg` choices; nothing before it can.","duration_ms":0}}

data: {"type":"response.output_item.done","item":{"type":"function_call_output","call_id":"call_48a61b02-b8e1-4305-81f3-23d8b09264ac","output":"  1  # CIBG Product Interpretation Framework\n  2  \n  3  ## Metadata\n  4  - Domain: cibg\n  5  - Category: products\n  6  - Tags: product-framework, interpretation, CASA, TD, lending, FX, trade-service, commentary\n  7  - Last updated: 2026-06-19\n  8  - Source: D1_Business_Rules-Metrics__CIBG_PRODUCTS.md\n  9  \n 10  ## Summary\n 11  A structured framework for interpreting CIBG product performance in business-finance analysis. For each product, defines the analytical lens: what it is, key metrics, core drivers, common distortions, and interpretation rules. Includes cross-product rules to prevent misleading conclusions.\n 12  \n 13  ## Detail\n 14  \n 15  ### How to Use This Framework\n 16  \n 17  For each product, the analyst should understand:\n 18  1. **Definition** — what the product represents commercially\n 19  2. **Primary metrics** — which KPIs assess performance\n 20  3. **Core business drivers** — what causes growth, decline, or volatility\n 21  4. **Common distortions** — what creates misleading short-term movements\n 22  5. **How to interpret changes** — what questions to ask before concluding\n 23  6. **Common analytical cuts** — which dimensions are most useful\n 24  \n 25  ### Universal Driver Framework\n 26  \n 27  All CIBG products share a common set of core drivers:\n 28  \n 29  | Driver | Deposit/Lending products | Other products (FX, Trade Service) |\n 30  | --- | --- | --- |\n 31  | Penetration (%) | % khách hàng sử dụng sản phẩm trên tổng khách hàng | % khách hàng sử dụng sản phẩm trên tổng khách hàng |\n 32  | Holding cus | Số lượng khách hàng đang nắm giữ sản phẩm | Số lượng khách hàng đang nắm giữ sản phẩm |\n 33  | Avg bal / Avg val | Số dư bình quân (Avg bal) | Giá trị giao dịch bình quân (Avg val) |\n 34  | %Margin | Biên lợi nhuận trên sản phẩm | Biên lợi nhuận trên sản phẩm |\n 35  \n 36  > **Decomposition logic:** Revenue = Holding cus × Avg bal (or val) × %Margin. Penetration drives Holding cus growth.\n 37  \n 38  ---\n 39  \n 40  ### 1. CASA (Current Account & Savings Account)\n 41  \n 42  **Definition:** Low-cost deposit balances in current/savings accounts. Treated as a balance product and key funding/relationship indicator.\n 43  \n 44  **Why it matters:**\n 45  - Supports low-cost funding\n 46  - Reflects transaction banking engagement\n 47  - Signals relationship depth and cash management quality\n 48  - Materially affects funding mix and margin economics\n 49  \n 50  **Primary metrics:** EOP bal, Avg bal, Net increase/decrease, Growth rate (MoM/QoQ/YoY), CASA share within total deposits, NTB vs existing-book CASA (by custype)\n 51  \n 52  **Core drivers:**\n 53  - Penetration (%) — tỷ lệ khách hàng CIBG có CASA trên tổng khách hàng\n 54  - Holding cus — số lượng khách hàng đang giữ CASA\n 55  - Avg bal — số dư bình quân mỗi khách hàng\n 56  - %Margin — biên lợi nhuận từ CASA (funding cost advantage)\n 57  \n 58  **Common distortions:**\n 59  - Temporary cash parking near period-end\n 60  - One-off inflows from project milestones\n 61  - Large short-lived balances from concentrated clients\n 62  - Month-end treasury/settlement timing effects\n 63  - Operational delays in outflows or internal transfers\n 64  \n 65  **Interpretation checklist:**\n 66  - Was the movement broad-based or concentrated in a few customers?\n 67  - Is the change structural or temporary timing effect?\n 68  - Did uplift come from existing customers or new business?\n 69  - Is the balance linked to a known pipeline event or one-off deal?\n 70  - Does average balance tell a different story from EOP balance?\n 71  \n 72  **Useful analytical cuts:** segment_group_cbc / segment, Sector / industry, custype (Existing vs New), area / cbc_lv1 / cbc_lv2\n 73  \n 74  > **Caution:** Do not treat all CASA growth as equally valuable. Sustainable operational CASA is more meaningful than short-lived concentration-driven balances.\n 75  \n 76  ---\n 77  \n 78  ### 2. TD (Term Deposit)\n 79  \n 80  **Definition:** Time-bound deposit balances placed for a defined tenor, usually at higher funding cost than CASA.\n 81  \n 82  **Why it matters:**\n 83  - Important funding volume source\n 84  - Supports balance-sheet growth or liquidity objectives\n 85  - More rate-sensitive than CASA\n 86  \n 87  **Primary metrics:** EOP bal, Avg bal, Net increase/decrease, Cost of funds / implied pricing, Mix by tenor, Mix by customer segment\n 88  \n 89  **Core drivers:**\n 90  - Penetration (%) — tỷ lệ khách hàng CIBG có TD trên tổng khách hàng\n 91  - Holding cus — số lượng khách hàng đang giữ TD\n 92  - Avg bal — số dư bình quân mỗi khách hàng\n 93  - %Margin — biên lợi nhuận từ TD (spread giữa lending yield và cost of TD)\n 94  \n 95  **Interpretation checklist:**\n 96  - Was growth price-led or relationship-led?\n 97  - Did balances come from new money or internal migration from CASA?\n 98  - Is the increase sustainable after campaign expiry?\n 99  - Are shorter-tenor balances driving volatility?\n100  \n101  > **Caution:** Growth in TD should not be seen as automatically positive if it materially worsens funding cost or cannibalizes CASA.\n102  \n103  ---\n104  \n105  ### 3. Lending\n106  \n107  **Definition:** Outstanding credit exposure or loan balances extended to customers.\n108  \n109  **Why it matters:**\n110  - Primary earning-asset driver\n111  - Core source of interest income\n112  - Strongly linked to risk, utilization, and portfolio quality\n113  \n114  **Primary metrics:** Outstanding balance, Average balance, Disbursement volume, Utilization / drawdown, Yield / spread, NIM contribution, New booking vs runoff\n115  \n116  **Core drivers:**\n117  - Penetration (%) — tỷ lệ khách hàng CIBG có dư nợ trên tổng khách hàng\n118  - Holding cus — số lượng khách hàng đang có dư nợ\n119  - Avg bal — dư nợ bình quân mỗi khách hàng\n120  - %Margin — biên lãi ròng (NIM/spread) trên danh mục cho vay\n121  \n122  **Common distortions:**\n123  - Backdated disbursement/booking effects\n124  - Short-term utilization spikes\n125  - Large bullet repayments\n126  - Refinancing/restructuring impacts\n127  - Product migration or portfolio transfer\n128  \n129  **Interpretation checklist:**\n130  - Was movement driven by new origination, utilization, or runoff?\n131  - Was balance change broad-based or from a few large names?\n132  - Did pricing or mix change alongside balance?\n133  - Is growth aligned with approved credit appetite and risk quality?\n134  \n135  > **Caution:** Loan growth without risk context or pricing context is incomplete. Balance growth should be interpreted together with margin and portfolio quality.\n136  \n137  ---\n138  \n139  ### 4. FX (Foreign Exchange)\n140  \n141  **Definition:** Customer foreign-exchange transactions and income generated from those flows.\n142  \n143  **Why it matters:**\n144  - Important fee and trading-related revenue source\n145  - Linked to customer trade activity and [[transaction-banking]] relationships\n146  - Sensitive to market conditions and customer flow intensity\n147  \n148  **Primary metrics:** FX income / revenue, Transaction volume, Number of active FX customers\n149  \n150  **Core drivers:**\n151  - Penetration (%) — tỷ lệ khách hàng CIBG có giao dịch FX trên tổng khách hàng\n152  - Holding cus — số lượng khách hàng active giao dịch FX\n153  - Avg val — giá trị giao dịch FX bình quân mỗi khách hàng\n154  - %Margin — biên spread/fee trên mỗi giao dịch FX\n155  \n156  **Common distortions:**\n157  - One-off large client deals\n158  - Market spikes not representative of recurring flow\n159  - Deal timing across periods\n160  - Spread compression or exceptional margin events\n161  \n162  **Interpretation checklist:**\n163  - Did revenue change because of flow volume, spread, or customer mix?\n164  - Was performance concentrated in a few clients?\n165  - Is the uplift market-driven or franchise-driven?\n166  - Did active customer penetration improve?\n167  \n168  > **Caution:** FX income volatility can be high. Strong performance in one period may not be recurring if driven by exceptional market conditions or one-off deals.\n169  \n170  ---\n171  \n172  ### 5. Trade Service (LC, LG, Collection, Cash Management)\n173  \n174  **Definition:** Products related to guarantees, letters of credit, collections, and cash management solutions.\n175  \n176  **Why it matters:**\n177  - Supports customer relationship depth\n178  - Generates fee and flow-based income\n179  - Often reinforces CASA and FX performance\n180  \n181  **Primary metrics:** Transaction volume, Fee income, Active customers, Product penetration, Balance linkage to CASA/settlement accounts\n182  \n183  **Core drivers:**\n184  - Penetration (%) — tỷ lệ khách hàng CIBG sử dụng Trade Service trên tổng khách hàng\n185  - Holding cus — số lượng khách hàng active sử dụng Trade Service\n186  - Avg val — giá trị giao dịch trade bình quân mỗi khách hàng\n187  - %Margin — biên fee/income trên mỗi giao dịch trade\n188  \n189  **Common distortions:**\n190  - Project-based trade spikes\n191  - Timing of shipment/settlement cycles\n192  - One-off documentary or guarantee issuance\n193  \n194  **Interpretation checklist:**\n195  - Is product usage deepening within existing customers?\n196  - Is activity linked to broader relationship growth?\n197  - Are fee and flow growth aligned, or is price driving the result?\n198  \n199  ---\n200  \n201  ### Cross-Product Interpretation Rules\n202  \n203  #### Rule 1: Separate structural vs temporary movements\n204  \n205  Every analysis must distinguish:\n206  - Recurring business growth\n207  - One-off transactional spikes\n208  - Timing effects\n209  - Concentration-driven changes\n210  \n211  #### Rule 2: Separate balance growth vs value growth\n212  \n213  Higher balances ≠ better economics:\n214  - CASA growth may be low quality if temporary\n215  - TD growth may worsen funding cost\n216  - Loan growth may dilute margin or increase risk\n217  \n218  #### Rule 3: Check breadth vs concentration\n219  \n220  Always assess whether results are:\n221  - Broad-based across customers and segments, or\n222  - Driven by a few names, deals, or short-lived events\n223  \n224  #### Rule 4: Connect products rather than analyzing in isolation\n225  \n226  Product reinforcement patterns:\n227  - CASA may improve with [[transaction-banking]] activation (domestic remittance)\n228  - FX may improve with overseas remittance, trade, and lending activity\n229  - Trade services often drive CASA stickiness\n230  \n231  ---\n232  \n233  ### Pre-Commentary Checklist\n234  \n235  Before writing a conclusion about ANY product, ask:\n236  1. What exactly is the KPI being discussed?\n237  2. Is the movement structural, seasonal, concentrated, or one-off?\n238  3. Which segment / sector / customer group drove the change?\n239  4. Is the observed movement commercially meaningful?\n240  5. Are there timing, pricing, accounting, or booking effects that may distort?\n241  6. Is there supporting evidence from related products or customer behavior?\n242  \n243  ---\n244  \n245  ### Commentary Guidance\n246  \n247  When explaining product performance:\n248  - Define the product briefly if needed\n249  - Describe what changed\n250  - Explain the most likely business drivers\n251  - State whether movement appears structural or temporary\n252  - Mention concentration or timing caveats\n253  - Suggest the next analytical check if confidence is limited\n254  \n255  ## See Also\n256  - [[transaction-banking]]\n257  - [[originate-to-distribution]]\n258  - [[cibg-customer-segmentation]]\n259  - [[cost-allocation]]\n260  \n261  ## Change History\n262  | Date | Change | Source |\n263  |------|--------|--------|\n264  | 2026-05-15 | Initial creation — product interpretation framework | CIBG_PRODUCTS.md |\n265  | 2026-06-19 | Standardize core drivers: Penetration, Holding cus, Avg bal/val, %Margin | User directive |","duration_ms":0}}

Defines product-level formulas for projecting NII, TOI, EOP Balance, and Active Customers across all major CIBG product lines: Interbank, Lending, Deposit, LC/Guarantee, Financial Markets, and Payment & Collection.\n 12  \n 13  ## Detail\n 14  \n 15  ### 1. Interbank Products\n 16  **PIC:** Maitt7\n 17  \n 18  | Driver | Formula |\n 19  |--------|---------|\n 20  | EOP Bal → Avg Bal → NII | NII = Avg Bal × Margin (hard margin from FM or market rate for Trade Loan TCB-funded) |\n 21  | Deposit from CIs | NII = Avg Bal × (%CI − %FTP) |\n 22  | Borrowing from CIs | NII = Avg Bal × Margin (Trade Loan margin = 0.2%, Syndication margin = 0.5%) |\n 23  | General | NII = Avg Bal × Margin, where Margin = (%CI − %FTP) |\n 24  \n 25  ### 2. Active Customer Model\n 26  **PIC:** Quynh + Thuy\n 27  \n 28  Active customer forecast uses a churn/reactivation model:\n 29  \n 30  ```\n 31  Active cus = EOP Active cus × (1 − %Churn cus)\n 32             + (%Reactivate × EOP Inactive Y-1)\n 33             + (Total NTB on-board × %Active)\n 34  ```\n 35  \n 36  Model outputs: Active cus count + MOA (Months of Activity).\n 37  \n 38  ### 3. Lending — Product Tree / Customer Tree\n 39  **PIC:** Anhptl2\n 40  \n 41  Decomposition dimensions:\n 42  - **CCY** (currency split)\n 43  - **Fix/Float** (rate type)\n 44  \n 45  | Driver | Formula |\n 46  |--------|---------|\n 47  | Bal movement → EOP Bal → Avg Bal | Linked via net change model |\n 48  | NII | NII = Avg Bal × (%CI + %FTP + %Int revert) |\n 49  | Pipeline | %Contribution (MTD) |\n 50  \n 51  #### EOP Balance Methodology\n 52  \n 53  Base-on net movement of last 3 months:\n 54  - If client has **3 continuous months** increase/decrease → use average of net change over 3 months\n 55  - If client did **NOT** have 3 continuous months increase/decrease → net change = 0 (keep flat)\n 56  \n 57  #### Lending Margin (Normal Lending + VND Loan with rate)\n 58  Margin section ref: 2.1 (Margin), 1 (Balance movement)\n 59  \n 60  ### 4. Deposit — Balance\n 61  **PIC:** taipt4\n 62  \n 63  Balance forecast methodology. ML models applied for CASA/Lending balance prediction.\n 64  \n 65  ### 5. LC Import / Domestic\n 66  **PIC:** linhtp5\n 67  \n 68  | Component | Formula |\n 69  |-----------|---------|\n 70  | NII Existing | Avg Bal × Margin |\n 71  | NII Momentum | Avg Bal × Margin |\n 72  | EOP Balance | EOP Bal Existing + EOP Bal Momentum |\n 73  | Avg Balance | Avg Bal Existing + Avg Bal Momentum |\n 74  | TOI | NII Existing + NII Momentum |\n 75  \n 76  ### 6. Normal Guarantee / SBLC\n 77  **PIC:** linhtp5\n 78  \n 79  | Component | Formula |\n 80  |-----------|---------|\n 81  | NII Existing | TOI Amortized (fee recognized over guarantee life) |\n 82  | NII Momentum | Avg Bal × Margin |\n 83  | EOP Balance | EOP Bal Existing + EOP Bal Momentum |\n 84  | Avg Balance | Avg Bal Existing + Avg Bal Momentum |\n 85  | TOI | NII Existing + NII Momentum |\n 86  \n 87  Note: Guarantee types include automatic/manual frequency — allocation method under review with VVIP team.\n 88  \n 89  ### 7. Financial Market Products\n 90  **PIC:** hadtt3 – hoangvt4\n 91  \n 92  | Product Type | Formula |\n 93  |--------------|---------|\n 94  | NTI (Trading) | NTI = Value × Spread / 1000 |\n 95  | Seasonality adjustment | Avg last 3M × Movement (%MoM LY by subsector_group) |\n 96  | NFI (Carried FWD) | NFI = NFI (Carried FWD) + (New Value × Spread / 1000) |\n 97  | NFI (Margin-based) | NFI = Avg Bal × Margin (1%) |\n 98  \n 99  ### 8. Payment + Collection\n100  **PIC:** maitt7 – hoangvt4\n101  \n102  | Driver | Formula |\n103  |--------|---------|\n104  | Volume | Vol = Active cus × Penetration × Ticket size |\n105  | Value | Val = Active cus × Penetration × Ticket size |\n106  \n107  ## See Also\n108  - [[cibg-product-framework]]\n109  - [[cibg-customer-segmentation]]\n110  - [[rorwa-methodology]]\n111  - [[cost-allocation]]\n112  \n113  ## Change History\n114  | Date | Change | Source |\n115  |------|--------|--------|\n116  | 2026-05-19 | Initial creation — full forecast logic tree for MMU segment | D3_Product-Pricing__Product_Tree__CIBG Forecast Logic 2026_MMU segment.md |","duration_ms":0}}

Segmentation is primarily driven by T24 SectorID classification and revenue thresholds.\n 12  \n 13  ## Detail\n 14  \n 15  ### 1. Segment Hierarchy\n 16  \n 17  The primary segmentation dimension classifies customers by size/type:\n 18  \n 19  | Segment | Definition | Revenue Threshold | Classification Basis |\n 20  | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n 21  | **VVIP** | Very high TOI, deep relationship with TCB | N/A (relationship-based) | CBC_lv2 mapping (VVIP1,3,5,5A,5B,6) |\n 22  | **LC** (Large Corporate) | Big size customers | Revenue > 2,000 bn VND | SectorID in (BIG, MMD, Treas) |\n 23  | **MM** (Mid Market) | Medium-large customers | Revenue 200–2,000 bn VND | Within MMU, not SME |\n 24  | **USME** (Upper SME) | Upper small-medium enterprises | Part of MMU segment | Within MMU, not SME |\n 25  | **SME** (Small-Medium Enterprise) | Smaller enterprises | Part of MMU segment | Specific SectorID classification |\n 26  | **Others** | Special/shared IDs | N/A | Special IDs (-1, -2, etc.) or shared accounting IDs |\n 27  \n 28  **Hierarchy levels:**\n 29  \n 30  ```\n 31  segment_group (LC | MMU | Others)\n 32    └─ segment_group_lv2 (LC | MMU | SME)\n 33        └─ segment (LC | MM | USME | SME | Others)\n 34  ```\n 35  \n 36  **Classification logic:**\n 37  - `segment_group`: LC = SectorID in (BIG, MMD, Treas); MMU = remaining; Others = special/shared IDs\n 38  - `segment_group_lv2`: Same as segment_group but splits MMU into MMU + SME\n 39  - `segment`: Most granular — splits MMU further into MM and USME\n 40  \n 41  ### 2. Area (Geographic)\n 42  \n 43  | Area | Description |\n 44  | --- | --- |\n 45  | **North** | Customers based in North region |\n 46  | **South** | Customers based in South region |\n 47  | **FI** | Financial Institution customers (not area-based) |\n 48  | **VVIPs** | VVIP customers (not area-based) |\n 49  | **Others** | Special/shared IDs (not area-based) |\n 50  \n 51  Area is determined by the CBC managing the customer (`cbc_area` field).\n 52  \n 53  ### 3. Sector Classification\n 54  \n 55  Economic sectors mapped via **GSO_ID** (General Statistics Office code):\n 56  \n 57  | Type | Sectors | Notes |\n 58  | --- | --- | --- |\n 59  | **Focus sectors** (5) | Real Estate, Infrastructure, Healthcare, Entertainment_Travel_Hospitality, Financial Services | Strategic priority — see [[cibg-sector-strategy]] |\n 60  | **Special: General ID** | Accounting/allocation purpose | Skip for business analysis |\n 61  | **Special: Diversified Sector** | Groups unfocused sectors | Large customer count, mixed industries |\n 62  \n 63  **Sector hierarchy:**\n 64  ```\n 65  sector (top level, e.g. \"Real Estate\")\n 66    └─ sub_sector (e.g. \"Construction\")\n 67        └─ sub_segment_gso / industry (e.g. \"Automotive spare parts commercialization\")\n 68  ```\n 69  \n 70  **Mapping priority:** GSO retag > GSO on T24 > Special customer mapping table\n 71  \n 72  ### 4. Enterprise Type\n 73  \n 74  | Type | Definition |\n 75  | --- | --- |\n 76  | **SOE** | State-Owned Enterprise (sở hữu nhà nước) |\n 77  | **FDI** | Foreign Direct Investment |\n 78  | **PRI** | Private (remaining) |\n 79  \n 80  **FDI sub-classification:** DDI (Domestic Direct Investment) vs FDI (Foreign)\n 81  \n 82  ### 5. Import/Export Classification\n 83  \n 84  | Category | Rule |\n 85  | --- | --- |\n 86  | **IMEX** | Has BOTH import AND export activity |\n 87  | **IMPORT** | Has import activity only (not IMEX) |\n 88  | **EXPORT** | Has export activity only (not IMEX) |\n 89  | **DOMESTIC** | No import/export activity |\n 90  \n 91  Activity indicators: Import_USD, Vol_ImpLC_USD, Vol_TTR_Outward_USD, Customer purchasing FX (for import side); Export_USD, Vol_ExpLC_USD, Customer sell FX (for export side).\n 92  \n 93  ### 6. Persona Logic\n 94  \n 95  Persona definition varies by segment:\n 96  \n 97  #### LC / MM / USME:\n 98  ```\n 99  Persona = \"{segment_TCB}\" – \"{FDI}\" & \"{country_group_en_L2}\"\n100  ```\n101  \n102  **Special case — Sector FS (Financial Services):**\n103  Uses GSO mapping → FI, Finance companies, Insurance, Securities, Funds, SBV, Public Sector, Others\n104  \n105  #### SME:\n106  ```\n107  Persona = \"{Eco Activity}\" - \"{IMEX}\" – \"{B2B-B2C}\"\n108  ```\n109  \n110  **16 SME personas:**\n111  \n112  | Eco Activity | IMEX | B2B/B2C |\n113  | --- | --- | --- |\n114  | Manufacturing | Domestic/Export/Import | B2B |\n115  | Retail | Domestic/Export/Import | B2C |\n116  | Service | Domestic/Export/Import | B2B or B2C |\n117  | Wholesale | Domestic/Export/Import | B2B |\n118  | Other | — | — |\n119  \n120  *If any of the 3 components (Eco Activity, IMEX, B2B-B2C) is missing → Persona defaults to \"Other\"*\n121  \n122  ### 7. Customer Archetypes\n123  \n124  Behavioral archetypes (binary flags, not mutually exclusive):\n125  \n126  | Archetype | Description |\n127  | --- | --- |\n128  | **Bidding for Growth** | Actively pursuing opportunities through bidding for contracts/projects |\n129  | **Cash Rich** | Significant cash reserves and strong cash flow; prioritize liquidity |\n130  | **Transaction Heavy** | High volume of financial transactions; need efficient processing |\n131  | **Credit Seeker** | Actively seeking credit/loans for expansion or new projects |\n132  | **Working Capital** | Need short-term financing to support daily operations and cash flow |\n133  | **MLT Capital Seeker** | Need medium/long-term credit for expansion + short-term working capital |\n134  \n135  ### 8. Active Customer Definition\n136  \n137  A customer is **Active** if they have interaction with CIBG products within the measurement window:\n138  \n139  | Flag | Window | Logic |\n140  | --- | --- | --- |\n141  | `active_3m` | 3 months | Primary active definition |\n142  | `active_1m` | 1 month | Short-term activity |\n143  | `active_12m` | 12 months | `active_3m = 1` at any point in last 12M |\n144  | `continuously_active` | YTD | Active every month from Dec prior year to reporting month |\n145  \n146  **Activity criteria by product:**\n147  \n148  | Product | Activity Indicator |\n149  | --- | --- |\n150  | Cash In/Out | Specific TranscodeIDs (1, 4, 11, 16, 29, 52, 61, 86, 201, 208, etc.) |\n151  | Tax, H2H, FEB | Transaction in window |\n152  | Cash Collection | NFI in window |\n153  | FX | VOL or NFX in window |\n154  | Lending, TD, CD | AVG Bal in window |\n155  | LC, LG, Trade Collection | VOL or AVG BAL or NFI in window |\n156  | Factoring | VOL or NFI in window |\n157  | Banca | APE or NFI in window |\n158  | Debit Card | Purchase/Withdraw transaction in window |\n159  \n160  ### 9. Customer Status Flags\n161  \n162  | Flag | Definition |\n163  | --- | --- |\n164  | **New Acquire** | Opened CIF in current fiscal year |\n165  | **New Reactive** | Was inactive (active_3M=0) at Dec prior year, now active |\n166  | **New customer** (`custype`) | Either New_Acquire=1 OR New_Reactive=1 |\n167  | **Churn** | Was active at Dec prior year (active_3M=1), now inactive |\n168  | **Credit customer** | Has Bal of Lending/LC/Guarantee or Limit in last 12M |\n169  | **Core flag** | TOI Annualised ≥ 0.2 bn VND |\n170  | **Focus** | Customer priority: Focus / Maintain / Others |\n171  | **MOA** | Main Operating Account (tagged by DA) |\n172  \n173  ### 10. B2B / B2C Classification\n174  \n175  Mapped by GSO code (from DA):\n176  - **B2B**: Business sells to other businesses\n177  - **B2C**: Business sells directly to end consumers\n178  - **Others**: Unclassified\n179  \n180  ## See Also\n181  - [[cibg-sector-strategy]]\n182  - [[cibg-coverage-model]]\n183  - [[cibg-cusinfo-data-dictionary]]\n184  - [[cost-allocation]]\n185  \n186  ## Change History\n187  | Date | Change | Source |\n188  |------|--------|--------|\n189  | 2026-05-15 | Initial creation — segmentation rules and dimensions | CIBG_CUSTOMER.md, Data Dictionary cibg d cusinfo.md |","duration_ms":0}}
